   366	std::optional<std::string> ValidateRecommendationPolicy(
   367	    const RecommendationPolicy& policy)
   368	{
   369	    if (!std::isfinite(policy.minimumLeaderScore) ||
   370	        !std::isfinite(policy.minimumInferenceAccuracy))
   371	        return "recommendation_policy_threshold_not_finite";
   372	    if (policy.maximumPredictedNeutralProportion &&
   373	        (!std::isfinite(*policy.maximumPredictedNeutralProportion) ||
   374	         *policy.maximumPredictedNeutralProportion < 0.0 ||
   375	         *policy.maximumPredictedNeutralProportion > 1.0))
   376	        return "maximum_predicted_neutral_out_of_range";
   377	    if (policy.topSourcesPerScope <= 0 ||
   378	        policy.maximumRecommendationsPerSource <= 0 ||
   379	        policy.maximumRecommendationsPerScan <= 0 ||
   380	        policy.minimumEvidenceCount <= 0 || policy.policyVersion <= 0)
   381	        return "recommendation_policy_positive_count_required";
   382	    if (policy.allowedParameters.empty())
   383	        return "recommendation_allowed_parameters_required";
   384	    const std::set<std::string> supported{
   385	        kCoreLrMult, kHeadLrMult, kLabelThreshold, kPredictionHorizon};
   386	    for (const std::string& name : policy.allowedParameters)
   387	        if (!supported.contains(name))
   388	            return "unsupported_recommendation_parameter:name=" + name;
   389	    if (policy.allowedParameters.contains(kCoreLrMult) &&
   390	        policy.coreLrOffsets.empty())
   391	        return "core_lr_offsets_required";
   392	    if (policy.allowedParameters.contains(kHeadLrMult) &&
   393	        policy.headLrOffsets.empty())
   394	        return "head_lr_offsets_required";
   395	    if (policy.allowedParameters.contains(kLabelThreshold) &&
   396	        policy.labelThresholdOffsets.empty())
   397	        return "label_threshold_offsets_required";
   398	    if (!policy.allowedParameters.contains(kCoreLrMult) &&
   399	        !policy.coreLrOffsets.empty())
   400	        return "core_lr_offsets_require_allowed_parameter";
   401	    if (!policy.allowedParameters.contains(kHeadLrMult) &&
   402	        !policy.headLrOffsets.empty())
   403	        return "head_lr_offsets_require_allowed_parameter";
   404	    if (!policy.allowedParameters.contains(kLabelThreshold) &&
   405	        !policy.labelThresholdOffsets.empty())
   406	        return "label_threshold_offsets_require_allowed_parameter";
   407	    if (policy.allowedParameters.contains(kPredictionHorizon) &&
   408	        !policy.allowHorizonChanges)
   409	        return "prediction_horizon_requires_explicit_enablement";
   410	    if (policy.allowHorizonChanges &&
   411	        !policy.allowedParameters.contains(kPredictionHorizon))
   412	        return "horizon_changes_require_prediction_horizon_parameter";
   413	    if (policy.allowHorizonChanges && policy.permittedHorizons.empty())
   414	        return "prediction_horizon_requires_permitted_horizons";
   415	    if (!policy.allowHorizonChanges && !policy.permittedHorizons.empty())
   416	        return "permitted_horizons_require_horizon_changes";
   417	    if (std::any_of(
   418	            policy.permittedHorizons.begin(), policy.permittedHorizons.end(),
   419	            [](int value) { return value <= 0; }))
   420	        return "permitted_horizons_must_be_positive";
   421	    const auto invalidOffset = [](const std::vector<double>& offsets) {
   422	        return std::any_of(offsets.begin(), offsets.end(), [](double value) {
   423	            return !std::isfinite(value) || value == 0.0;
   424	        });
   425	    };
   426	    if (invalidOffset(policy.coreLrOffsets)) return "invalid_core_lr_offset";
   427	    if (invalidOffset(policy.headLrOffsets)) return "invalid_head_lr_offset";
   428	    if (invalidOffset(policy.labelThresholdOffsets))
   429	        return "invalid_label_threshold_offset";
   430	    if (policy.expirationDays && *policy.expirationDays <= 0)
   431	        return "recommendation_expiration_must_be_positive";
   432	    return std::nullopt;
   433	}
   434	
   435	std::string CanonicalRecommendationDouble(double value)
   436	{
   437	    if (!std::isfinite(value))
   438	        throw std::invalid_argument("recommendation_identity_nonfinite_number");
   439	    if (value == 0.0) value = 0.0; // Canonicalize IEEE negative zero.
   440	    std::array<char, 128> buffer{};
   441	    const auto result = std::to_chars(
   442	        buffer.data(), buffer.data() + buffer.size(), value,
   443	        std::chars_format::general);
   444	    if (result.ec != std::errc{})
   445	        throw std::runtime_error("recommendation_identity_number_format_failed");
   446	    return std::string(buffer.data(), result.ptr);
   447	}
   448	
   449	std::string RecommendationCanonicalHash(const std::string& canonicalText)
   450	{
   451	    return StableRecommendationHash(canonicalText);
   452	}
   450	inference accuracy are present and finite, its evidence count and both metrics
   451	meet policy minima, and any required predicted-neutral proportion is present,
   452	finite, within ``[0,1]``, and at or below the configured maximum. Rejections
   453	use stable machine-readable reasons and do not throw away the underlying policy
   454	or identity validation detail.
   455	
   456	``GenerateRecommendationCandidates`` supports only these mutations:
   457	
   458	* additive offsets to ``core_lr_mult``;
   459	* additive offsets to ``head_lr_mult``;
   460	* additive offsets to ``label_threshold``; and
   461	* explicit permitted ``prediction_horizon`` values, only when horizon changes
   462	  are enabled and the parameter is allowlisted.
   463	
   464	Learning-rate multipliers and thresholds follow the queue/worker launch
   465	contract: results must be finite, positive, and different from the source.
   466	Horizons must be positive and different from the source. No campaign-specific
   467	upper or lower caps are invented. A nullable source multiplier is never
   468	guessed; each requested mutation is rejected as ``missing_source_value``.
   469	Symbol, target epochs, all train/inference dates, checkpoint interval, and
   470	resume model are copied unchanged.
   471	
   472	Every emitted candidate differs from the normalized source semantic
   473	configuration in exactly one field. Both semantic and invocation identities
   474	are built through the Step 1 canonical identity APIs. Structural distance
   475	contains absolute delta, relative delta when the source is nonzero, and signed
   476	horizon delta for horizon changes; it is metadata only and is not a score.
   477	
   478	Offsets and horizons are sorted and deduplicated before construction. Valid
   479	candidates are ordered by parameter (core LR, head LR, threshold, horizon),
   480	numeric proposed value, semantic canonical text, then invocation canonical
   481	text. All valid candidates are constructed and sorted before the per-source
   482	limit retains the first N; overflow candidates are returned as
   483	``per_source_limit`` rejections. There is intentionally no cross-source limit
   484	in this pure step.
   485	
   486	In-memory duplicate handling follows the Step 1 collision contract. Semantic
   487	canonical text is authoritative. Hash buckets accelerate lookup, but every
   488	hash match is checked by canonical text. Equal canonical text is rejected as a
   489	duplicate; equal hashes with different canonical text retain both candidates
   490	and produce an explicit collision record.
   491	
   492	Deferred work
   493	-------------
   494	
   495	Scoring, PostgreSQL evidence loading, database duplicate lookup,
   496	collision-aware persistence, migrations, CLI inspection, scheduler scans,
   497	approval, and experiment conversion remain deferred. Before approval/queueing,
   498	future work must persist or validate a complete implementation/feature contract
   499	for behavior-affecting settings that the experiment row cannot currently
   500	reconstruct.
   315	    if (request.requestedMaximum && *request.requestedMaximum <= 0)
   316	        throw std::invalid_argument("recommendation_maximum_must_be_positive");
   317	
   318	    pqxx::connection connection{connectionString};
   319	    if (!RecommendationSchemaExists(connection))
   320	        throw std::runtime_error(
   321	            "recommendation schema required; run ./migrate_lstm_db.sh");
   322	
   323	    RecommendationScanRequest scanRequest;
   324	    scanRequest.policy = request.policy;
   325	    scanRequest.filters.symbol = request.symbol;
   326	    scanRequest.filters.predictionHorizon = request.predictionHorizon;
   327	    scanRequest.filters.sourceExperimentId = request.sourceExperimentId;
   328	    scanRequest.requestedMaximum = request.requestedMaximum;
   329	    const long long scanId = BeginRecommendationScan(connection, scanRequest);
   330	    RecommendationScanCounters counters;
   331	    output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_SCAN_START"
   332	           << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   333	           << ",policy_hash=" << RecommendationPolicyHash(request.policy)
   334	           << ",symbol=" << OptionalText(request.symbol)
   335	           << ",horizon=" << OptionalNumber(request.predictionHorizon)
   336	           << ",source_experiment_id="
   337	           << OptionalNumber(request.sourceExperimentId)
   338	           << ",requested_maximum=" << OptionalNumber(request.requestedMaximum)
   339	           << '\n';
   340	    output.flush();
   341	
   342	    try
   343	    {
   344	        const std::vector<RecommendationSourceLoadResult> loaded =
   345	            LoadRecommendationSources(connection, scanRequest.filters);
   346	        counters.sourcesScanned = static_cast<int>(loaded.size());
   347	        std::vector<RecommendationSource> eligibleSources;
   348	        for (const RecommendationSourceLoadResult& item : loaded)
   349	        {
   350	            if (!item.source)
   351	            {
   352	                ++counters.sourcesSkipped;
   353	                output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_SOURCE_SKIPPED"
   354	                       << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   355	                       << ",source_experiment_id=" << item.experimentId
   356	                       << ",reason=" << RecommendationMachineText(item.skipReason) << '\n';
   357	                continue;
   358	            }
   359	            const RecommendationSourceEligibilityResult eligibility =
   360	                EvaluateRecommendationSource(request.policy, *item.source);
   361	            if (!eligibility.eligible)
   362	            {
   363	                ++counters.sourcesSkipped;
   364	                output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_SOURCE_SKIPPED"
   365	                       << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   445	                work.push_back(CandidateWork{
   446	                    selected.source, selected.groupKey,
   447	                    selected.rankWithinGroup, static_cast<int>(index + 1),
   448	                    std::move(generated.candidates[index])});
   449	            }
   450	        }
   451	
   452	        std::sort(work.begin(), work.end(), CandidateWorkLess);
   453	        const int scanLimit = request.requestedMaximum.value_or(
   454	            request.policy.maximumRecommendationsPerScan);
   455	        if (work.size() > static_cast<std::size_t>(scanLimit))
   456	        {
   457	            for (std::size_t index = static_cast<std::size_t>(scanLimit);
   458	                 index < work.size(); ++index)
   459	            {
   460	                ++counters.candidatesRejected;
   461	                output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_CANDIDATE_REJECTED"
   462	                       << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   463	                       << ",source_experiment_id="
   464	                       << work[index].source.experimentId
   465	                       << ",parameter="
   466	                       << RecommendationMutationParameterText(
   467	                              work[index].candidate.parameter)
   468	                       << ",proposed_value="
   469	                       << CanonicalRecommendationDouble(
   470	                              work[index].candidate.proposedValue)
   471	                       << ",reason=scan_limit" << '\n';
   472	            }
   473	            work.resize(static_cast<std::size_t>(scanLimit));
   474	        }
   475	
   476	        for (std::size_t index = 0; index < work.size(); ++index)
   477	        {
   478	            const int structuralRank = static_cast<int>(index + 1);
   479	            const CandidateWork& item = work[index];
   480	            output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_CANDIDATE"
   481	                   << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   482	                   << ",source_experiment_id=" << item.source.experimentId
   483	                   << ",source_rank=" << item.sourceRank
   484	                   << ",parameter="
   485	                   << RecommendationMutationParameterText(
   486	                          item.candidate.parameter)
   487	                   << ",source_value="
   488	                   << CanonicalRecommendationDouble(item.candidate.sourceValue)
   489	                   << ",proposed_value="
   490	                   << CanonicalRecommendationDouble(
   491	                          item.candidate.proposedValue)
   492	                   << ",semantic_hash=" << item.candidate.semanticIdentity.hash
   493	                   << ",invocation_hash="
   494	                   << item.candidate.invocationIdentity.hash
   495	                   << ",policy_hash="
   496	                   << RecommendationPolicyHash(request.policy)
   497	                   << ",structural_rank=" << structuralRank << '\n';
   498	            try
   499	            {
   500	                RecommendationPersistenceRequest persistence;
   501	                persistence.recommendationScanId = scanId;
   502	                persistence.policy = request.policy;
   503	                persistence.source = item.source;
   504	                persistence.candidate = item.candidate;
   505	                persistence.sourceRank = item.sourceRank;
   506	                persistence.generationOrdinal = item.generationOrdinal;
   507	                persistence.structuralRank = structuralRank;
   508	                const RecommendationPersistResult persisted =
   509	                    PersistRecommendationIdempotently(connection, persistence);
   510	                counters.hashCollisions += static_cast<int>(
   511	                    persisted.recommendationMatch.hashCollisions.size());
   512	                for (const RecommendationDuplicateMatch::HashCollision& observed :
   513	                     persisted.recommendationMatch.hashCollisions)
   514	                {
   515	                    const RecommendationCandidateHashCollision& collision =
   516	                        observed.collision;
   517	                    output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_HASH_COLLISION"
   518	                           << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   519	                           << ",source_experiment_id="
   520	                           << item.source.experimentId
   521	                           << ",identity_kind=" << observed.identityKind
   522	                           << ",hash=" << collision.hash
   523	                           << ",first_canonical="
   524	                           << RecommendationMachineText(collision.firstCanonicalText)
   525	                           << ",second_canonical="
   526	                           << RecommendationMachineText(collision.secondCanonicalText) << '\n';
   527	                }
   528	                switch (persisted.outcome)
   529	                {
   530	                    case RecommendationPersistOutcome::created:
   531	                        ++counters.recommendationsCreated;
   532	                        output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_CREATED"
   533	                               << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   534	                               << ",recommendation_id="
   535	                               << *persisted.recommendationId
   536	                               << ",source_experiment_id="
   537	                               << item.source.experimentId
   538	                               << ",semantic_hash="
   539	                               << item.candidate.semanticIdentity.hash << '\n';
   540	                        break;
   541	                    case RecommendationPersistOutcome::existingExperiment:
   542	                        ++counters.duplicatesExistingExperiment;
   543	                        output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_DUPLICATE"
   544	                               << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   545	                               << ",source_experiment_id="
   546	                               << item.source.experimentId
   547	                               << ",duplicate_type=existing_experiment"
   548	                               << ",matched_experiment_id="
   549	                               << OptionalNumber(
   550	                                      persisted.experimentMatch.experimentId)
   551	                               << ",invocation_exact="
   552	                               << (persisted.experimentMatch.invocationExactMatch
   553	                                      ? 1 : 0) << '\n';
   554	                        break;
   555	                    case RecommendationPersistOutcome::terminalExperiment:
   556	                        ++counters.duplicatesTerminalExperiment;
   557	                        output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_DUPLICATE"
   558	                               << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   559	                               << ",source_experiment_id="
   560	                               << item.source.experimentId
   561	                               << ",duplicate_type=excluded_terminal_experiment"
   562	                               << ",matched_experiment_id="
   563	                               << OptionalNumber(
   564	                                      persisted.experimentMatch.experimentId)
   565	                               << '\n';
   566	                        break;
   567	                    case RecommendationPersistOutcome::activeRecommendation:
   568	                        ++counters.duplicatesActiveRecommendation;
   569	                        ++counters.recommendationsAlreadyExisting;
   570	                        output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_ALREADY_EXISTS"
   571	                               << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   572	                               << ",recommendation_id="
   573	                               << OptionalNumber(persisted.recommendationId)
   574	                               << ",source_experiment_id="
   575	                               << item.source.experimentId
   576	                               << ",duplicate_type=active_recommendation"
   577	                               << ",invocation_exact="
   578	                               << (persisted.recommendationMatch.invocationExactMatch
   579	                                      ? 1 : 0) << '\n';
   580	                        break;
   581	                    case RecommendationPersistOutcome::historicalRecommendation:
   582	                        ++counters.duplicatesHistoricalRecommendation;
   583	                        output << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_DUPLICATE"
   584	                               << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   585	                               << ",source_experiment_id="
   586	                               << item.source.experimentId
   587	                               << ",duplicate_type=historical_recommendation"
   588	                               << ",matched_recommendation_id="
   589	                               << OptionalNumber(persisted.recommendationId)
   590	                               << '\n';
   591	                        break;
   592	                }
   593	            }
   594	            catch (const std::exception& error)
   595	            {
   596	                output.flush();
   597	                ++counters.persistenceErrors;
   598	                errors << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_PERSISTENCE_ERROR"
   599	                       << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   600	                       << ",source_experiment_id=" << item.source.experimentId
   601	                       << ",semantic_hash="
   602	                       << item.candidate.semanticIdentity.hash
   603	                       << ",error=" << RecommendationMachineText(error.what()) << '\n';
   604	            }
   605	        }
   606	        CompleteRecommendationScan(connection, scanId, counters);
   607	        PrintScanCounters(output, "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_SCAN_COMPLETE",
   608	                          scanId, counters);
   609	        return counters.persistenceErrors == 0 ? 0 : 1;
   610	    }
   611	    catch (const std::exception& error)
   612	    {
   613	        ++counters.persistenceErrors;
   614	        try
   615	        {
   616	            FailRecommendationScan(connection, scanId, counters, error.what());
   617	        }
   618	        catch (const std::exception& finalizationError)
   619	        {
   620	            errors << "EXPERIMENT_RECOMMENDATION_SCAN_FINALIZATION_FAILED"
   621	                   << ",scan_id=" << scanId
   622	                   << ",original_error="
   623	                   << RecommendationMachineText(error.what())
   624	                   << ",finalization_error="
   625	                   << RecommendationMachineText(finalizationError.what())

     1	Phase 6A Recommendation Campaign Follow-Up Proposal
     2	====================================================
     3	
     4	Purpose and entity
     5	------------------
     6	
     7	Phase 6A defines ``RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposal``: one pure,
     8	immutable, deterministic advisory candidate for later operator review.  The
     9	name deliberately distinguishes this value from Phase 4D campaign
    10	materialization, which is an explicit persisted operation over an approved
    11	campaign.  A Phase 6A proposal materializes nothing.
    12	
    13	Exact immutable inputs
    14	----------------------
    15	
    16	The builder accepts exactly one Phase 5A
    17	``RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessment`` contract version 2 and one Phase
    18	5C ``RecommendationCampaignOutcomePolicyDecision`` contract version 1.  The
    19	decision must embed a Phase 5C policy contract version 1.  Phase 6A consumes
    20	only their public immutable typed values; it does not consume Step 5B formatted
    21	output, a database row, repository metadata, a scheduler value, a CLI request,
    22	or mutable operator state.
    23	
    24	Eligibility prerequisite
    25	------------------------
    26	
    27	Construction requires the validated Step 5C decision to report all of:
    28	
    29	* ``followUpEligibility == EligibleForOperatorReview``;
    30	* ``followUpAuthorized == false``; and
    31	* ``campaignInterpretation == Favorable``.
    32	
    33	Phase 6A does not derive those values, inspect Step 5A metric truth tables, or
    34	recalculate Step 5C evidence sufficiency, direction, interpretation, or
    35	eligibility.  It copies the exact decision classifications after validation.
    36	Eligibility for operator review remains neither approval nor authorization.
    37	
    38	Alignment and fail-closed validation
    39	------------------------------------
    40	
    41	Before construction, the builder validates supported assessment, policy, and
    42	policy-decision versions and each authoritative-canonical/tagged-hash pair.
    43	The decision's embedded assessment version, canonical text, and hash must
    44	equal the supplied assessment exactly.  Hash equality is never substituted
    45	for canonical-text equality.
    46	
    47	Campaign approval ID, campaign canonical identity and hash, complete
    48	materialization identity and approval linkage, member counts, and every
    49	ordered member identity must match exactly.  Member identity includes ordinal,
    50	materialization-member ID, ranking-member ID, recommendation ID, source-
    51	experiment ID, proposal ID, and optional expected-experiment ID.  Mismatch,
    52	malformed identity, unsupported version, non-eligibility, non-favorable
    53	interpretation, or authorization fails closed with a stable explicit reason.
    54	The narrow plain-data validation view exists only for safe malformed-boundary
    55	tests and cannot construct any upstream value, proposal identity, or proposal.
    56	Stable boundary reasons include ``assessment_contract_unsupported``,
    57	``policy_contract_unsupported``, ``policy_decision_contract_unsupported``,
    58	``assessment_identity_mismatch``, ``policy_identity_mismatch``,
    59	``policy_decision_identity_mismatch``, ``campaign_identity_mismatch``,
    60	``materialization_identity_mismatch``, ``member_count_mismatch``,
    61	``member_identity_mismatch``, ``decision_not_eligible_for_operator_review``,
    62	``favorable_campaign_interpretation_required``, and
    63	``follow_up_authorization_must_be_false``.
    64	
    65	Immutable output and non-authority
    66	----------------------------------
    67	
    68	The output exposes the proposal contract version; exact assessment, policy,
    69	and policy-decision version/canonical/hash triples; complete campaign and
    70	materialization identities; exact ordered members and count; copied Step 5C
    71	evidence sufficiency, campaign interpretation, eligibility, and false
    72	authorization; and the stable provenance reason
    73	``eligible_favorable_policy_decision``.
    74	
    75	The type fixes these semantics at compile time and in canonical identity:
    76	``readOnly=true``, ``databaseFree=true``, ``persistent=false``,
    77	``advisory=true``, ``authoritative=false``, ``approved=false``,
    78	``activated=false``, ``executionAuthorized=false``,
    79	``followUpAuthorized=false``, ``schedulerWork=false``, and
    80	``declaresCampaignSuccess=false``.
    81	
    82	Canonical identity grammar
    83	--------------------------
    84	
    85	Contract version 1 canonical text begins with
    86	``experiment_recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_v1``.  Semicolon-
    87	named fields bind, in order:
    88	
    89	* proposal version and every fixed safety semantic;
    90	* assessment, policy, and policy-decision version/canonical/hash triples;
    91	* campaign approval ID/canonical/hash;
    92	* materialization ID, approval linkage, contract version, member count,
    93	  canonical text, and hash;
    94	* each upstream-canonical ordered member identity, including
    95	  ``expected_experiment_id``;
    96	* Step 5C evidence sufficiency, campaign interpretation, follow-up
    97	  eligibility, false decision authorization, and proposal reason.
    98	
    99	Every embedded canonical string and other delimiter-capable text is framed as
   100	``BYTE_LENGTH:VALUE``.  Integer and length formatting uses the classic locale.
   101	Canonical text is authoritative; the existing tagged FNV-1a hash is only an
   102	accelerator.  ``observedAt``, clocks, PID, CLI formatting, repository metadata,
   103	and any future approval or activation state are excluded.  Consequently,
   104	observation time cannot change identity, while every behaviorally relevant
   105	upstream identity can.
   106	
   107	Canonical-size limit
   108	--------------------
   109	
   110	Version 1 permits at most 1,048,576 canonical-text bytes.  The builder checks
   111	the completed canonical byte count before identity and proposal construction
   112	and throws ``proposal_canonical_size_exceeded`` above that inclusive ceiling.
   113	The bound limits a future review/persistence payload deliberately; it neither
   114	adds persistence nor predicts a later database schema.  Any later change to
   115	the ceiling or grammar requires a new contract version or an explicitly
   116	compatible architecture decision.
   117	
   118	Non-goals and later Phase 6 boundaries
   119	--------------------------------------
   120	
   121	Phase 6A adds no SQL, migration, table, repository, service, formatter, CLI,
   122	preview command, operator-review persistence, approval, rejection, activation,
   123	execution, scheduler work, queue, worker, campaign, recommendation, experiment,
   124	or Phase 4D materialization.  It does not claim profitability, statistical
   125	significance, repeatability, campaign success, or follow-up authorization.
   126	
   127	Persistence, explicit operator review, approval/rejection, activation, and
   128	execution remain distinct possible Phase 6B--6F concerns.  This contract does
   129	not authorize, prescribe, or partially implement any of them.
   196	### 4.2 Control flow
   197	
   198	Completed evidence → deterministic source selection → pure candidates →
   199	collision-aware persistence → optional explicit score/evaluation/ranking. The
   200	Phase 4C proposal → review → paused execution → activation chain consists of
   201	separately invoked and audited manual actions. Phase 4D reads ranking and Phase
   202	4C history to plan and review; campaign approval records only an explicit human
   203	decision and adds no implicit arrow to mutation or execution.
   204	
   205	The governed campaign path is:
   206	
   207	```text
   208	recommendation ranking
   209	-> read-only campaign plan
   210	-> read-only campaign review
   211	-> explicit persisted campaign approval
   212	-> explicit approved-campaign materialization to Phase 4C proposals
   213	-> read-only exact campaign handoff
   214	-> explicit atomic Phase 4C proposal review for all exact campaign members
   215	-> either optional Phase 5 Step 1 execution then optional Step 2 activation
   216	-> or optional Phase 5 Step 3 atomic execution-and-activation launch
   217	-> existing scheduler lifecycle for ordinary pending experiments
   218	-> optional read-only Phase 5 Step 4 operational status snapshot
   219	-> optional read-only Step 5b outcome assessment
   220	-> optional pure Step 5c advisory policy interpretation
   221	-> optional pure Phase 6A follow-up proposal for later operator review
   222	-> optional exact Phase 6B persistence and read-only operator preview
   223	-> optional explicit Phase 6C administrative approval or rejection
   224	-> optional independent Phase 6D governance ratification of advancement
   225	```
   226	
   227	All displayed stages through exact proposal review are implemented through
   228	Phase 4D Step 6; Phase 4C execution and activation remain separately invoked
   229	actions. Phase 5 Step 1 invokes only the existing Phase 4C execution operation
   230	for the exact campaign membership. Phase 5 Step 2 separately invokes only the
   231	existing Phase 4C activation operation; neither step invokes the other.
   232	Phase 5 Step 3 is a separate command that reuses both transaction-bound
   233	authorities inside one transaction; it does not call either public command.
   234	Phase 5 Step 4 reads the immutable materialization and bounded downstream
   235	evidence only; it neither invokes Steps 1–3 nor contacts the scheduler process.
   236	Step 5b reuses that status lifecycle inside one read-only snapshot. Step 5c
   237	contains no repository or CLI and consumes only an already-built immutable
   238	assessment. Phase 6A consumes only the exact immutable assessment and policy
   239	decision and terminates at a non-authorizing proposal value. Phase 6B may
   240	persist and preview only that exact value; it introduces no downstream action
   241	arrow. Phase 6C may record only one administrative decision and likewise adds
   242	no downstream action arrow. Phase 6D may record only one separate governance
   243	ratification of advancement after an eligible Phase 6C review and also adds no
   244	downstream action arrow or Phase 6E capability.
   245	
   246	### 4.3 Ownership boundaries
   247	
   248	Canonical text decides identity; repository transactions decide persistence;
   249	pure scoring, planning, campaign-review, outcome-policy, and follow-up-proposal
   250	components decide advisory output; pure review rules decide legal transitions;
   251	and the operator separately
   252	supplies review, conversion, and activation actions. Only Phase 4C Steps 4–5
   253	may create or activate the one provenance-linked experiment. Campaign
   254	planning, review, approval, outcome assessment, outcome policy, Phase 6A
   255	follow-up proposal, Phase 6B persistence/preview, and Phase 6C administrative
   256	review/presentation, and Phase 6D governance ratification never do.
   257	
   258	## 5. Data model
   530	## 11. Future extensions
   531	
   532	### 11.1 Approved extension points
   533	
   534	Versioned policies, pure candidate/scoring components, typed repository/service
   535	APIs, and separate inspection commands.
   536	
   537	### 11.2 Deferred capabilities
   538	
   539	Experiment queueing/execution from conversion, multi-source attribution,
   540	automatic research campaigns, profitability evidence, and scheduler-managed
   541	recommendation work. Phase 4C Step 1 provides the pure proposed-
   542	specification contract, Step 2 provides durable proposal audit evidence, and
   543	Step 3 provides explicit append-only manual proposal review. Step 4 permits one
   544	explicit approved proposal to create one paused experiment. Step 5 permits a
   545	separate explicit operator action to make that exact experiment pending under
   546	the existing scheduler lifecycle.
   547	
   548	### 11.3 Required decisions
   549	
   550	ADR-0005 defines explicit conversion authorization, implementation identity,
   551	idempotent experiment creation, transactions, and audit history. Approval alone
   552	does not invoke conversion. Budgets, queueing, execution, and scheduler capacity
   553	remain deferred under ADR-0004.
   554	
   555	Phase 4C Step 2 adds the append-only
   556	``experiment_recommendation_conversion_proposal`` record and a narrow
   557	repository. Exact canonical text remains authoritative; same-hash distinct
   558	canonical identities receive separate collision ordinals, while concurrent
   688	persists an assessment, declares campaign success, or authorizes follow-up.
   689	
   690	Phase 5 Step 5c adds a pure versioned policy over the Step 5a assessment. It
   691	uses the assessment's existing classifications and deltas, applies explicit
   692	metric-direction and advisory evidence-coverage rules, and separates evidence
   693	sufficiency, conservative interpretation, and eligibility for a later explicit
   694	operator review. Only a favorable, sufficient, all-comparable campaign is
   695	eligible for such review; neutral, unfavorable, mixed, and inconclusive
   696	campaigns are not. Its result is non-persistent and non-authoritative, always
   697	records follow-up authorization as false, and adds no repository, CLI,
   698	scheduler, worker, schema, or lifecycle behavior.
   699	
   700	Phase 6A adds the distinct
   701	``RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposal`` contract over one exact Step 5a
   702	assessment v2 and one exact Step 5c policy decision v1 containing policy v1.
   703	It validates authoritative canonical text as well as hashes and exact campaign,
   704	materialization, count, and ordered-member alignment. Only an eligible,
   705	favorable, explicitly non-authorizing decision yields a proposal. Contract v1
   706	binds every safety semantic and exact upstream canonical identity, excludes
   707	``observedAt``, and rejects canonical text above 1,048,576 bytes before
   708	construction. The proposal is not approval, persistence, activation,
   709	execution, scheduler work, success evidence, or authority for later phases.
   710	
   711	Phase 6B persists only that already-built immutable proposal. One append-only
   712	manifest and ordered member set preserve every Phase 6A field. Exact canonical
   713	text remains authoritative, collision ordinals and timestamps stay outside
   714	identity, and reload reruns the Phase 6A payload/canonical invariants. Preview
   715	uses one repeatable-read, read-only transaction and explicitly reports false
   716	approval, activation, execution, authorization, queue, scheduling, worker, and
   717	experiment mutation state. Phase 6B adds no operator decision or action path.
   718	
   719	Phase 6C records only one immutable administrative ``approved`` or ``rejected``
   720	event for one exact persisted Phase 6B proposal. The review canonical binds the
   721	proposal ID/version/canonical/hash, decision, reviewer, reason, and fixed
   722	negative action semantics. Exact retry is idempotent; every changed payload for
   723	that proposal conflicts. Approval grants only possible later consideration and
   724	does not activate, execute, authorize follow-up, queue, schedule, contact the
   725	scheduler, launch workers, create/modify experiments, or declare success.
   726	
   727	Phase 6D separately records one immutable ``ratified`` governance event for
   728	advancement after one exact persisted Phase 6C approved review. Its canonical
   729	binds the complete review and proposal identity, reviewer, fixed role,
   730	distinct ratifier, basis, and separation policy. Exact retry is idempotent;
   731	every changed payload conflicts. The service loads rather than reconstructs
   732	the authoritative upstream chain. Phase 6D adds no CLI, Phase 6E, activation,
   733	execution, follow-up authorization, scheduler, worker, experiment, model,
   734	continuation, or success capability.
   735	
   736	## 12. References
     1	Phase 4D Step 1 Deterministic Recommendation Campaign Planning
     2	================================================================
     3	
     4	Purpose and boundary
     5	--------------------
     6	
     7	Step 1 produces a reproducible, read-only answer to: which members of one
     8	explicit persisted recommendation-ranking snapshot would be selected for a
     9	bounded future campaign, and why? It creates no campaign row, proposal,
    10	review, execution, activation, experiment, scheduler action, or worker.
    11	
    12	The command is an explicit operator request. The policy defaults to disabled
    13	in the domain; ``--plan-recommendation-campaign`` is the opt-in that enables
    14	it. The scheduler does not poll plans or Phase 4C audit tables.
    15	
    16	Authoritative evidence
    17	----------------------
    18	
    19	The planner requires one completed Phase 4B ranking snapshot by primary key.
    20	Its immutable member order and final scores are authoritative. Recommendation
    21	rows supply persisted source metrics and display/configuration identities.
    22	Phase 4C workflow state is loaded through the existing Step 6 derivation, so
    23	campaign planning does not redefine review or lifecycle semantics.
    24	Copied ranking-member source experiment, symbol, horizon, and family values are
    25	also checked against the authoritative recommendation row. A mismatch is
    26	retained and excluded as ``identity_validation_failed``.
    27	
    28	The repository uses one PostgreSQL read transaction and a fixed number of
    29	set-based queries. It reads at most 1001 ranking members to enforce the hard
    30	1000-candidate boundary. No production statement uses mutation SQL, row locks,
    31	advisory locks, or sequences. Existing indexes are sufficient; Step 1 adds no
    32	migration or privilege.
    33	
    34	Policy version 1
    35	----------------
    36	
    37	The versioned policy contains:
    38	
    39	* an explicit enabled flag;
    40	* maximum selected and considered counts;
    41	* minimum persisted leader score and inference accuracy;
    42	* maximum persisted predicted-neutral proportion;
    43	* an optional minimum profitability threshold;
    44	* optional per-symbol and per-horizon limits;
    45	* a per-source-experiment limit, conservatively one by default;
    66	--------------------------------
    67	
    68	Candidates retain the ranking snapshot's ``global_ordinal`` order. A
    69	recommendation ID, ranking member ID, and finally complete canonical evidence
    70	are deterministic fallbacks if malformed evidence duplicates an earlier key.
    71	Filters preserve the original durable positions.
    72	
    73	Policy, metric, ranking-bucket, identity, workflow, and candidate-limit rules
    74	are evaluated before group and campaign limits. Group limits are filled by
    75	higher-ranked included candidates. The stored recommendation invocation
    76	canonical is the pre-conversion target identity used to prevent two selected
    77	candidates from describing identical future work; it is not conversion
    78	authorization. Every considered row remains in the plan with exactly one
    79	``include`` or ``exclude`` decision and a deterministic, de-duplicated reason
    80	sequence. A zero-selection plan is successful.
    81	
    82	Leader score and inference accuracy are required persisted fields. The source
    83	neutral proportion is nullable in historical storage; a missing value fails
    84	closed with ``predicted_neutral_metric_unavailable`` instead of silently
    85	passing the configured maximum.
    86	
    87	Workflow exclusions distinguish pending review, rejection, approval without
    88	execution, paused execution, activation/scheduler ownership, completion,
    89	failure, cancellation, and inconsistent evidence. Reconsideration flags only
    90	relax their named rejected/failed/cancelled exclusions. Inconsistent workflows
    91	are always excluded.
    92	
    93	Canonical plan identity
    94	-----------------------
    95	
    96	The authoritative campaign-plan canonical text length-frames variable text and
    97	contains the contract version, complete campaign-policy canonical, scope
    98	canonical, ranking-snapshot canonical/hash, every bounded candidate's persisted
    99	evidence and Phase 4C workflow provenance, final decision, and ordered reason
   100	codes. Its hash uses the existing recommendation canonical hash helper and is
   101	only an accelerator.
   102	
   103	The display generation timestamp is deliberately excluded. Input row order,
   104	connection information, host, process, and locale do not affect identity.
   105	Changing policy, scope, ranking membership/evidence, workflow evidence,
   146	Deferred work
   147	-------------
   148	
   149	Phase 4D Step 2 consumes this plan through a separate deterministic, read-only
   150	review contract. Step 3 may persist one explicit operator decision only after
   151	reconstructing the plan and review from an explicit snapshot and matching the
   152	expected review identity. Plan persistence, proposal creation, bulk review, campaign
   153	execution, automatic activation, background scans, budgets, durable
   154	profitability policy, and scheduler integration remain outside Step 1.
   155	Phase 4D Step 4 does not mutate the plan: it reconstructs the exact approved
   156	plan and materializes only its selected members as Phase 4C proposals.
   157	
   158	References
   159	----------
   160	
   161	* ``docs/Phase4BExperimentRecommendationRanking.rst``
   162	* ``docs/Phase4CExperimentRecommendationConversionWorkflow.rst``
   163	* ``docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignReview.rst``
   164	* ``docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignApproval.rst``
   165	* ``docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignMaterialization.rst``
   166	* ``docs/architecture/Volume_VIII_Recommendation_Engine.md``
   167	* ``docs/architecture/Volume_IX_Trading_Profitability.md``
     1	Phase 4D Step 3 Explicit Recommendation Campaign Approval
     2	==========================================================
     3	
     4	Purpose and boundary
     5	--------------------
     6	
     7	Step 3 records one immutable human decision for one exact deterministic
     8	campaign review. The decision is ``approved`` or ``rejected``. It is audit
     9	evidence for possible future campaign execution; it does not create a
    10	proposal, conversion, activation, experiment, scheduler action, or worker.
    11	
    12	An operator must explicitly supply the ranking-snapshot ID, the expected
    13	campaign-review identity hash, reviewer identity, reason, and the complete
    14	Step 1 planning policy and scope. Scores, ranks, plan contents, and a zero-
    15	selection review never imply approval.
    16	
    17	Stale-evidence protection
    18	-------------------------
    19	
    20	The write service opens one PostgreSQL transaction, loads the explicit
    21	completed ranking snapshot and Phase 4C workflow evidence, reconstructs the
    22	Step 1 plan, reconstructs and validates the Step 2 review, and compares the
    23	operator's expected review hash exactly. The approval is inserted in that same
    24	transaction. An identity mismatch, missing snapshot, conflict, validation
    25	failure, or database error rolls the transaction back.
    26	
    27	The approval row stores the authoritative snapshot, planning-policy, planning-
    28	scope, plan, and review canonical evidence and hashes; the Step 2 contract
    29	version and complete summary counts; the ordering-verification result; and the
    30	explicit decision, reviewer, and reason. The database timestamp is audit
    31	metadata and is excluded from semantic identity.
    32	
    33	Canonical identity and duplicate behavior
    34	-----------------------------------------
    35	
    36	Approval contract version 1 length-frames every variable string and binds all
    37	authorization-relevant provenance, summary fields, decision, reviewer, and
    38	reason. The existing recommendation canonical hash is an indexed accelerator;
    39	canonical text is authoritative.
    40	
    41	One terminal decision is allowed per exact campaign-review canonical identity.
    42	An identical retry returns the original row as ``existing_identical``. Reusing
    43	the same review with a changed decision, reviewer, reason, or other persisted
    44	payload returns a conflict and never replaces history. A real hash collision
    45	uses a separate collision ordinal. A transaction-scoped advisory lock only
    46	serializes that hash bucket while exact canonical equality decides identity.
    47	Concurrent identical requests therefore converge on one durable row.
    48	
    49	A valid zero-selection review may be rejected for audit purposes. It cannot be
    50	approved.
    51	
    52	Schema and privileges
    53	---------------------
    54	
    55	Migration 040 creates
    56	``experiment_recommendation_campaign_approval``. The ranking-snapshot foreign
    57	key is restrictive. Summary, decision, version, text-size, and zero-selection
    58	constraints fail closed, and every stored identity hash must use the canonical
    59	``fnv1a64:`` plus 16-lowercase-hex form. Canonical columns use the bytewise
    60	``C`` collation.
    61	Exact review-canonical lookup uses a PostgreSQL hash index so the potentially
    62	large authoritative text is not stored in a size-limited btree key; PostgreSQL
    63	still rechecks exact canonical equality.
    64	Runtime role ``pqxx`` receives ``SELECT``, column-limited payload ``INSERT``,
    65	and only sequence ``USAGE``. It cannot insert the generated ID or timestamp,
    66	and receives no ``UPDATE``, ``DELETE``, or ``TRUNCATE`` privilege. No trigger
    67	modifies an experiment or any prior recommendation/conversion evidence.
    68	
    69	CLI
    70	---
   114	Deferred work
   115	-------------
   116	
   117	Campaign execution, bulk proposal creation/review, activation, profitability-
   118	aware authorization, background scans, and scheduler integration remain
   119	outside Step 3. Campaign approval records explicit human authorization for one
   120	exact deterministic campaign review. It does not create or modify experiments
   121	and does not execute the campaign.
   122	
   123	Phase 4D Step 4 may consume an ``approved`` row only to materialize the exact
   124	selected members as Phase 4C conversion proposals. Proposal review, execution,
   125	activation, experiment creation, and scheduling remain separate boundaries.
   126	
   127	References
   128	----------

   131	* ``docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignReview.rst``

    66	8. Implement only after acceptance unless Volume I §16.4 applies.

The strongest fit is a planning bridge, not an action phase: a ratified proposal should become an exact, bounded plan for re-entering the existing advisory recommendation pipeline from the campaign’s result experiments. That preserves the established proposal→decision→materialization boundaries and avoids creating a second experiment or scheduler path. I’m finalizing the authority, replay, and concurrency contract, including why the current recommendation scan writer is not yet a safe direct consumer.
Repository contracts and candidate directions have been compared. The recommended increment is a read-only, deterministic planning bridge from Phase 6D ratification back into the existing advisory recommendation pipeline, with operational materialization deferred to a separately authorized phase.

# Architectural recommendation

The next phase should be:

**Phase 6E — Ratified Follow-Up Recommendation-Generation Planning**

Phase 6E should be a deterministic, bounded, read-only planning bridge from a Phase 6D ratification back into the existing advisory Phase 4A recommendation pipeline. It should not authorize execution, persist recommendations, create experiments, activate lifecycle state, or interact with the scheduler.

This is the smallest increment that gives Phase 6D a useful downstream consequence without inventing operational authority.

## 1. Recommended phase name

**Phase 6E — Ratified Follow-Up Recommendation-Generation Planning**

Suggested version-1 intent:

`generate_advisory_recommendations_from_result_experiments`

The action kind should be fixed in version 1 rather than accepting an open-ended “follow-up type.” Replication, continuation, date-window extension, or arbitrary campaign execution would need their own contracts.

## 2. Problem it solves

Phase 6D answers whether an independently reviewed proposal may advance, but it deliberately does not say what advancement does. The Phase 6A proposal contains exact prior campaign provenance and each member’s optional `expectedExperimentId`, but it contains no:

- new experiment specification;
- mutation policy;
- candidate membership;
- budget;
- requested recommendation scan;
- queue or scheduler request; or
- execution identity.

That makes direct authorization or activation impossible without guessing. Phase 6E should close this semantic gap by producing an exact answer to:

> Given this ratified follow-up proposal and an explicit bounded recommendation policy, which advisory recommendation candidates would be generated from the prior campaign’s exact result experiments, and why?

The result is an action-specific proposal, not an action.

## 3. Why it belongs after Phase 6D

Phase 6D explicitly ratifies only advancement into a separately controlled phase and grants no Phase 6E capability ([Phase 6D contract](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase6DRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalRatification.rst:13>)). Phase 6E would establish its own narrowly scoped planning capability, with the persisted ratification as a prerequisite.

The resulting progression remains consistent with the foundation’s evidence-before-automation and proposal/decision/execution separation ([Volume I](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/architecture/Volume_I_Foundation.md:62>)):

```text
Phase 6B persisted proposal
  ├─ Phase 6C rejected → terminal
  └─ Phase 6C approved
       └─ Phase 6D independently ratified
            └─ Phase 6E deterministic plan preview
                 ─X─ no automatic persistence or execution
```

Phase 6D therefore authorizes entry into planning; it does not authorize whatever plan is later produced.

## 4. Authority boundaries

Phase 6E should own only:

- Validation of one exact persisted Phase 6D ratification.
- Mapping each exact Phase 6A member’s result experiment into a prospective Phase 4A recommendation source.
- Application of an explicit versioned recommendation policy.
- Deterministic source selection and candidate generation.
- Read-only duplicate and eligibility classification.
- One immutable canonical plan identity and presentation.

Its input should include:

- `ratificationEventId`;
- expected Phase 6D ratification identity hash;
- an explicit enabled follow-up planning policy;
- an embedded existing `RecommendationPolicy` canonical identity;
- explicit hard source and candidate bounds.

The plan should use the members’ `expectedExperimentId` values as the proposed new recommendation sources. That mapping is the principal new semantic decision and must be explicit in the ADR. Phase 6A already binds these IDs in ordered membership ([Phase 6A contract](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase6ARecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposal.rst:47>)).

Phase 6E must not:

- Recompute or reinterpret the Phase 5 outcome policy.
- Parse presentation output.
- Treat the Phase 6D ratification as approval of a subsequently chosen mutation policy.
- Persist a recommendation scan or recommendation rows.
- Reuse the original source experiment when an exact result experiment is required.
- Materialize Phase 4C proposals.
- Create, modify, pause, or activate experiments.
- Signal or start the scheduler.
- Introduce a generic arbitrary-action adapter.
- Claim repeatability, profitability, statistical significance, or campaign success.

It should reuse the existing Phase 4A candidate-generation rules rather than cloning them. Those rules already define supported single-parameter mutations and deterministic ordering ([Phase 4A foundation](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase4AExperimentRecommendationFoundation.rst:456>)).

## 5. State-machine changes

No Phase 6A–6D row or status should be mutated.

Phase 6E should add one derived eligibility state:

- `ratified_planning_eligible`

It should not add a durable `planned` flag. A read-only plan does not consume, supersede, or complete the ratification.

One ratification may produce zero or more plan previews under different explicit policies. This is desirable at the planning stage: the first policy an operator happens to try must not silently become authoritative.

Recommended plan outcomes include:

- `valid_with_candidates`
- `valid_zero_candidates`
- `source_ineligible`
- `source_evidence_unavailable`
- `duplicate_existing_experiment`
- `duplicate_active_recommendation`
- `duplicate_historical_recommendation`
- `ratification_identity_mismatch`
- `upstream_provenance_invalid`
- `plan_bound_exceeded`

As in Phase 4D planning, a zero-candidate plan may be a valid inspection result, but a future approval phase should not allow it to be approved for materialization ([Phase 4D precedent](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignPlanning.rst:68>)).

## 6. Ownership model

The existing layering should remain:

```text
CLI/presentation
  → Phase 6E planning service
    → Phase 6E read-only snapshot repository
      → PostgreSQL

Planning service
  → existing Phase 4A pure source-selection/candidate-generation components
```

Specific ownership:

- **Phase 6D repository:** Owns validated ratification hydration and the complete upstream chain.
- **Phase 6E repository:** Owns bounded, set-based loading of exact result experiment, model, analysis, and duplicate evidence.
- **Phase 6E domain:** Owns the plan contract, canonicalization, ordering, caps, stable reasons, and fixed non-authority semantics.
- **Phase 4A domain:** Continues to own mutation eligibility and candidate construction.
- **PostgreSQL:** Remains authoritative for persisted ratification and source evidence.
- **Experiment lifecycle and scheduler:** Own nothing in Phase 6E.

Raw Phase 6D or experiment rows must not escape repositories, and Phase 6E must not issue direct SQL outside its repository.

## 7. Transaction boundaries

Phase 6E should use one bounded:

`REPEATABLE READ, READ ONLY`

transaction to load:

1. The exact Phase 6D ratification.
2. The transitively validated Phase 6C/6B chain.
3. Ordered Phase 6A members.
4. Exact result experiments and their supported source evidence.
5. Current duplicate recommendation/experiment evidence.

The repository should use fixed-count, set-based queries and explicit ordering. It should acquire:

- no advisory locks;
- no tuple locks;
- no sequence values;
- no write transaction.

After loading the complete typed snapshot, the transaction can close before potentially larger canonicalization work. The plan is then calculated from that immutable snapshot.

Any future approval or materialization must be a different phase and transaction. It should reconstruct the Phase 6E plan and compare its full expected identity inside the write transaction, following the existing Phase 4D stale-evidence pattern ([Phase 4D approval](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignApproval.rst:17>)).

## 8. Concurrency considerations

Because Phase 6E is read-only:

- Concurrent identical plans do not conflict.
- Plans built from the same ratification, policy, and PostgreSQL snapshot must have identical canonical identities.
- Ordinary recommendation or experiment activity should not be blocked.
- Repeatable-read prevents mixed evidence within one plan.
- A plan may become stale immediately after the transaction ends.

A later write phase must recheck:

- ratification identity;
- source evidence identity;
- candidate canonical identities;
- current duplicate state;
- expected plan canonical text.

Concurrent insertion of a matching recommendation after planning should cause a deterministic reclassification or stale-plan rejection during future materialization—not duplicate creation.

## 9. Replay and determinism requirements

The plan identity should bind:

- Phase 6E contract version and fixed intent.
- Ratification event ID and complete ratification canonical/hash.
- Embedded recommendation-policy canonical/hash.
- Explicit hard bounds.
- Exact ordered proposal-member-to-result-experiment mapping.
- Source experiment, model, analysis, semantic, and invocation evidence.
- Source eligibility and selection results.
- Every included and excluded candidate.
- Candidate semantic and invocation canonical identities.
- Duplicate classification and matched authoritative IDs.
- Stable ordered reason codes.
- Fixed negative authority semantics.

It should exclude:

- observation time;
- database connection/session information;
- local process ID;
- CLI formatting;
- row-return order;
- generated display timestamps.

Canonical text remains authoritative; hashes remain accelerators. Same-hash/different-canonical values must remain distinct and observable.

The ADR should establish an explicit canonical-size limit and hard caps before allocating candidate collections. A limit of at most 1,000 considered sources and 1,000 plan candidates would align with the repository’s existing campaign safety boundary, though the exact value should be accepted in the ADR.

## 10. Scheduler interaction

None.

Phase 6E should not:

- add a scheduler work class;
- insert a pending experiment;
- queue a recommendation scan;
- consume capacity;
- start a worker;
- signal a running scheduler;
- poll Phase 6 tables.

A future materialized recommendation would still need to traverse the established recommendation review, ranking, Phase 4D campaign, Phase 4C conversion, and Phase 5 activation path before an experiment becomes ordinary scheduler-eligible work.

Any direct scheduler integration would require a separate ADR satisfying the full trigger, claim, capacity, retry, recovery, shutdown, and observability contract ([Volume I scheduler rules](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/architecture/Volume_I_Foundation.md:345>)).

## 11. Separation-of-duties implications

The existing Phase 6C reviewer/Phase 6D ratifier separation remains unchanged.

No additional mandatory actor separation is needed for Phase 6E because planning is read-only and records no durable decision. The planner’s identity should not enter scientific plan identity merely because someone previewed it.

A later phase that approves or materializes an exact plan must separately decide:

- whether the plan author may approve it;
- whether the Phase 6D ratifier may also approve the exact Phase 6E plan;
- whether a third distinct actor is operationally required.

The safe default is that choosing a post-ratification mutation policy must not become self-authorization. That decision should be explicit in the later action-authority ADR rather than smuggled into Phase 6E.

## 12. Risks

- **Ambiguous follow-up meaning.** Mapping result experiments into Phase 4A generation is the most repository-aligned interpretation, but it is not presently accepted behavior. ADR-0010 must make that meaning explicit.
- **Recursive research amplification.** A favorable campaign could repeatedly produce new plans. Mitigate with explicit invocation, fixed caps, no scheduler polling, and no automatic plan materialization.
- **Stale duplicate evidence.** Plans are point-in-time. Future writes must reconstruct and revalidate.
- **Canonical growth.** Phase 6D canonical payloads can already exceed two MiB; binding sources and candidates could grow materially. Define caps and size checks before construction.
- **Incomplete implementation identity.** Phase 4A documents behavior-affecting settings that cannot yet be reconstructed. Phase 6E must preserve the same fail-closed boundary and must not claim exact replay beyond the supported identity contract ([Phase 4A limitation](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase4AExperimentRecommendationFoundation.rst:492>)).
- **Partial recommendation-scan persistence.** The existing generation command starts a scan before source loading and catches per-candidate persistence failures while continuing ([current generation service](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Sources/ExperimentRecommendationService.cpp:323>)). It also supports only one optional source-experiment filter. It is therefore not yet an appropriate atomic Phase 6D consumer. Phase 6E should reuse its pure algorithms, not invoke its write orchestration.
- **False authority language.** Output must state that no recommendations, experiments, queues, or workers were created.
- **Governance status drift.** ADR-0009 is still marked `Proposed`, despite the Phase 6D implementation and reported independent review ([ADR index](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/architecture/adr/README.md:70>)).

## 13. Alternatives considered

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Direct Phase 6E activation/execution | Reject. No exact new experiment or campaign specification exists, and this would bypass Phase 4C/5 authority. |
| Copy or relaunch the previous materialization | Reject. Reusing old proposals describes old work, not a defined follow-up, and would collide with existing identities. |
| Invoke the existing recommendation scan writer directly | Defer. It lacks exact multi-source ratification provenance and permits partial per-candidate persistence. |
| Generic “follow-up authorization” row | Reject. It would repeat Phase 6D governance without defining an exact action. |
| Begin autonomous research automation | Reject for this increment. Volume X requires budgets, reservations, stop controls, recovery, and multiple new ADRs. |
| End permanently at Phase 6D | Safe but incomplete. Operators could act manually, but the action would have no typed linkage to the ratification. |
| Name this Phase 7A instead | Reasonable, but Phase 6A documentation already treats later Phase 6 steps as separately gated concerns, and Phase 6D explicitly names Phase 6E. Continuing with Phase 6E is less disruptive. |

## 14. Recommended implementation sequence

1. **Close Phase 6D governance first.** If the independent review constituted formal acceptance, change ADR-0009 from `Proposed` to `Accepted` and align the Volume VIII/XII status text. Otherwise Phase 6D remains non-authoritative under the repository’s ADR rules.

2. **Draft ADR-0010:** “Ratified follow-up planning re-enters the advisory recommendation pipeline.”

3. **Define the pure Phase 6E contract:**
   - fixed `generate_advisory_recommendations_from_result_experiments` intent;
   - ratification prerequisite;
   - embedded policy;
   - bounds;
   - canonical grammar;
   - reasons and safety flags.

4. **Add pure tests first:** golden canonical/hash, locale independence, ordering, collision behavior, bounds, malformed provenance, missing result experiments, and zero-candidate plans.

5. **Add the read-only evidence snapshot repository:** batched exact-ID queries, repeatable-read snapshot, source and duplicate validation.

6. **Build the planner by composing existing Phase 4A functions.** Do not clone candidate mutation rules or invoke the current scan writer.

7. **Add a read-only service and preview.** A CLI may follow, but it should remain an explicit standalone inspection command with no `--yes` write path.

8. **Add repository/concurrency tests:** unchanged sequences and tables, no locks, snapshot consistency, deterministic stale evidence, and bounded query behavior.

9. **Run focused regressions and the incremental Release build** after confirming scheduler safety.

10. **Defer later authorities:**
    - Phase 6F: exact plan review/approval and separation-of-duties decision.
    - Phase 6G: idempotent materialization into advisory recommendation scan/recommendation provenance.
    - Existing Phase 4A–5 workflows remain the only path toward experiments and scheduler work.

## 15. Should an ADR precede implementation?

**Yes—unambiguously.**

ADR-0010 must be accepted before Phase 6E implementation because the increment defines:

- a new semantic mapping from result experiments to recommendation sources;
- a new canonical identity;
- a new downstream ownership boundary;
- stale-evidence and replay rules;
- safety caps;
- future recommendation-materialization provenance.

Volume I explicitly requires an ADR for identity, ownership, transaction, lifecycle, conversion, interface, and security changes, and says implementation should not begin while the required ADR remains merely proposed ([Volume I governance](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/architecture/Volume_I_Foundation.md:529>)).

## Review record

- Files changed: none.
- Behavioral change: none; recommendation only.
- Tests/builds run: none, because no implementation or repository modification was requested.
- Read-only inspection: ADRs, Phase 4A–6D documentation, domain/service/repository code, migrations, tests, git history, and worktree status.
- Remaining key assumption: reviewers must explicitly accept that “follow-up” version 1 means generating advisory recommendations from each prior member’s result experiment.
- `git status --short`: clean.
- `git diff --stat`: empty.
- Reviewed branch/commit: `phase6e` at `d78771f`.
